#+title: Review: Growing Conversation Prompt Cache Remediation
#+date: 2026-10-05
#+startup: overview

* Status

In-review.  Deterministic implementation acceptance and external cache benefit
are separate.  No shipped adapter advertises efficient cache-plus-curation
support; unconfirmed curation stays unavailable.

* Scope and authority

DP1 implements F98-G1 and F98-C1--C6 in [[file:plan.org][the reviewed plan]].
The admitted plan identity is recorded in [[file:plan-review.org][plan-review.org]].  Review base is
=39407dd76f50612cf65ae851aa8bb4fc54f67880=.  Provider adapters own wire policy;
existing harness/lifetime owners enforce semantic availability and commit.
No runtime cache registry, durable cache state, user enable switch, or hit-rate
heuristic was added.  The running Emacs and audited Board/store remain untouched.

* Review 1: Pre-acceptance risk review

An independent read-only reviewer found three actionable defects in the first
settled implementation diff.  This was not a fixed-point decision.

1. F98-C6: disabling wire publication left curation labels and prepared effects
   available.  Corrected by gating labels, tool observation presentation,
   request admission, preflight, and commit against adapter-derived capability.
2. F98-C6: caller-supplied OpenAI tools could republish =context-curate=.
   Corrected at both Responses and Chat Completions composition boundaries.
3. F98-C5/C6: a classifier accepted repeated static hits as P3 success.
   Corrected with ordered curation/rebuilt-history/reuse phases, a static-only
   baseline, two cache-read advances per phase, repeated curation, and a material
   summary/erase phase.  No boolean or legacy =pass= string grants P3 support.

Independent closure review remains pending.

* Architecture self-check

1. If this package stopped here, would the project be better off?  It removes
   fixed-only cache policy and prevents unproved curation effects; provider
   benefit still requires external evidence.
2. Final direction: API-capability-owned caching with unchanged-history reuse
   and efficient curation admission.
3. Stable requirements are semantic projection and caller inability to weaken
   caching.  Optional provider syntax and capability proof remain adapter-local.
4. Adapters own syntax; core lifetime/loop owners own presentation and effects.
5. Coupling is reduced by removing harness cache-key and caller-policy APIs.
6. Core depends on backend semantic capabilities, never provider wire fields.
7. Network/auth/cache side effects remain in adapters and opt-in test runners.
8. Added derived capability values live per request; provider caches are
   disposable and accepted session projections remain the only history truth.
9. Automatic boundaries allow new history writes, whose cost is necessary to
   make later history reusable.  No measured latency or cost claim is made.
10. The semantic capability interface reports unavailable support explicitly;
    ordinary chat remains usable without a reserved carrier.
11. Stale caller controls are stripped; old persisted records remain readable.
12. Removed cache defaults/key inheritance, explicit-only selection, factory
    options, and obsolete test policy assertions.
13. Unsupported effects fail at admission/preflight/commit; provider errors
    remain provider errors, and missing cache evidence remains unknown.
14. Credential-free request, forced-effect, lifetime, replay, and classifier
    tests prove owner behavior; public harness composition gates prove joins.

** Findings

*** TODO Medium/P2 R2-F1: Optional retention overrides unqualified organization capability

F98-C2 requires unknown optional controls to use the endpoint's compatible
automatic path.  =e-openai-profile-cache-retention= at
=lisp/adapters/openai/e-openai-profile.el:526--536= returns ="24h"= for listed
older first-party Responses models using provider, endpoint, model, and wire
API alone.  There is no organization capability qualification.  The renderer
always emits that derived value at =e-openai-responses.el:703--709=.

The current official OpenAI prompt-caching guide distinguishes model support
from organization availability: older models supporting both policies default
to extended retention without ZDR and to in-memory retention with ZDR, and the
guide requires verifying model and organization policies before selecting a
value.  Thus this branch demonstrably overrides an organization-dependent
provider default without evidence that the organization supports the override.
The model list proves syntax/model support only.  An HTTP rejection was not
observed or established by the documentation, and is not claimed here.

Source authority: [[https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching][OpenAI prompt-caching guide]],
Cache lifetime / Retention defaults and Zero Data Retention, and Summary of
model differences (consulted 2026-10-05).  This supports provider design only;
it provides no gateway or configured-account acceptance evidence.

Smallest owning correction: omit optional retention while organization
capability is unknown and preserve the provider's eligible default.  The
documented non-ZDR default already selects extended retention; the provider
selects the eligible default for other organizations.  Keep stable internally
derived routing keys for older supported first-party requests.  Add no caller
policy or organization override.  Update the public request witness currently
expecting unconditional =24h= (=e-openai-continuation-composition-test.el:675=)
and diagnostics to describe the provider-selected default.  Parent independently
accepted this capability-boundary correction; the issue is actionable without
introducing a runtime registry or retry framework.

*** TODO Medium/P2 R2-F2: Growing evidence compares Anthropic fresh input instead of total context

F98-C5 requires provider-specific total and uncached inputs to stay distinct.
=e-anthropic--usage-item= at =lisp/adapters/anthropic/e-anthropic.el:1451--1477=
correctly emits fresh =:input-tokens= and total
=:context-input-tokens= as input plus read plus creation, only when all are
known.  OpenAI's =:input-tokens= is already provider total.  However the new
configured harness probe joins usage unchanged at
=e2e/e-cache-efficiency-e2e-test.el:106--115=, and
=e-live-e2e--classify-growing-cache-evidence= at
=e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el:1313--1330= requires strictly growing raw
=:input-tokens=.  P3 calls the same classifier.

A credential-free probe using the actual Anthropic normalizer and actual
classifier produced:

#+begin_example
total context:  18000 30000 42000
fresh input:    18000 12000 12000
cache reads:        0 18000 30000
creation:           0     0     0
classifier: "history-not-growing"
#+end_example

The endpoint/model/static identity was held fixed.  This is efficient growing
reuse, but the public evidence composition rejects it because fresh input falls
then stays flat.  When Anthropic total cannot be derived, fresh input is also not
an honest substitute for missing total.  Parent independently verified the
normalizer/join/classifier composition.

Smallest owning correction: normalize evidence once at the shared classifier
boundary using provider total context; retain raw fresh/read/creation separately.
Use Anthropic's known context total (or its exact known constituent sum), and
OpenAI's already-total input.  Missing required total remains unavailable;
never fill absent counters with zero or require nonexistent OpenAI write counts
to establish OpenAI total.  Strengthen the public usage join tests with actual
provider-normalized payloads, decreasing/equal fresh input, and missing totals.
This corrects acceptance evidence rather than altering production usage events.

*** TODO Medium/P2 R2-F3: P3 qualification does not prove static attribution or repeated keep continuity

F98-C5/C6 and plan P3 (=plan.org:306--322=) require reads attributable to
unchanged conversation history through repeated projection-preserving curation.
Only a material summary/erase rewrite may miss at its changed boundary; keep
must not repeatedly make unchanged history cold.  The shared P3 classifier at
=e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el:1342--1428= has two proven counterexamples at the same
qualification boundary.

First, the initial =stable-only-baseline= accepts a cold or partly warm read
count at =1376--1379=.  Subsequent reads only need exceed that observed count
at =1394--1396=, which is not a static-token upper bound.  A static-only input
of 4,096 tokens with zero baseline reads, followed by accepted keep and summary
phases each using inputs =10000,14000,18000= and reads =0,1024,2048=, returns
=pass=.  Every read can fit entirely within the 4,096-token static prefix.
Matching provider/static/history fingerprints and ordered phases do not make
those reads conversation reuse.

Second, a warm 4,096-token static baseline followed by =keep1,keep2,summary1=
also returns =pass= when every rebuilt request reads only 4,096 tokens, then
later subturns read 8,192 and 12,288.  Inputs grow across phases
=(10000,14000,18000), (22000,26000,30000), (34000,38000,42000)=.  Both keep
phases have the same unchanged-history fingerprint.  The second keep therefore
drops the preceding warm 12,288-token read to the static 4,096-token read, then
rewarm proceeds.  The classifier ignores immediate rebuilt-request reuse and
cross-keep continuity; it accepts exactly the recurring cache loss P3 forbids.
The pure temporal probe is =/private/tmp/e-feature98/review2-temporal-probe.el=.

These are acceptance false positives, not proof that any current production
adapter advertises curation: all shipped curation declarations remain
none/unconfirmed.  They are still material because this helper can qualify
future capability evidence.  Parent verified and accepted both counterexamples.

Smallest owning correction: qualify a conservative static-only token/cache
ceiling with matching accounting and identity; cold or partial reads cannot
serve as that ceiling.  Require history-attributed reuse beyond it and repeated
state-only/keep transitions retaining the previously reusable unchanged history
at the immediate request, subject to provider granularity.  Distinguish those
transitions from material summary/erase rebuilds, which may warm again.  Prove
the repeated-keep phase and material rebuild phases separately, using the P2
append-only control.  Preserve missing or incomplete attribution as unconfirmed.
Strengthen classifier contract fixtures with both counterexamples and genuine
positive continuity evidence; a single keep-plus-summary warming sequence is
insufficient.

** Architecture self-check

1. The package improves deterministic policy and unavailable-curation safety;
   the three findings prevent accepting it as fixed-point yet.
2. It moves toward provider-capability-owned growing reuse with semantic
   projection as independent truth.
3. Provider optional syntax and proof are uncertain, local and reversible;
   R2-F1 removes an unqualified assumption rather than adding abstraction.
4. Adapters own cache syntax/capability; core loop/lifetime owns publication,
   admission and effects; shared E2E helpers own qualification accounting.
5. Removed caller policy APIs reduce coupling.  New interfaces describe real
   capability variation and do not add general cache management.
6. Core consumes backend semantic capabilities; no core dependency on provider
   wire fields or concrete adapters was introduced.
7. Network/auth/provider-cache effects remain at adapter/test-runner boundaries.
8. Derived policy is request-local, cache state remains disposable, and existing
   durable projections remain authoritative.  No cache registry was added.
9. Automatic boundaries may incur writes for growing history; measured cost/
   latency is unproved.  P3 must reject repeat cold-history rebuilds as R2-F3 shows.
10. Ordinary chat works without a carrier; unsupported effects are explicitly
    rejected.  All production substitutions currently expose no curation.
11. Stale controls cannot alter policy and old persisted records remain readable;
    incompatible continuation shapes fall back to authorized complete replay.
12. Obsolete public cache defaults, inheritance, explicit-only selection and
    factory assignments are removed; no migration switch is retained.
13. Unsupported effects fail at admission/preflight/commit; missing cache
    counters remain unknown.  Provider failures remain visible rather than
    silently claiming benefit.
14. Credential-free request/replay/effect tests prove semantic owners.  Actual
    accounting/classifier counterexamples challenge public evidence joins.
    Corrections belong to local adapter/evidence owners and narrow contract tests.

No new architectural defect was confirmed outside the three owning corrections.
The selected glossary scope remains unchanged; no new public term required a
glossary change.  No architectural cleanup was relabeled as required remediation.

* Review 2 remediation

R2-F1 removes the unqualified retention selector and optional wire field.
Older first-party requests retain their stable internal routing key and report
=provider-default= retention; organization eligibility stays with the provider.
No new caller control or capability registry is introduced.

R2-F2 normalizes total input at the shared evidence boundary while preserving
raw fresh/read/creation counters.  Actual Anthropic usage payloads with falling
then flat fresh input correctly establish growing total context and cache reuse;
incomplete totals remain unavailable.  OpenAI needs no cache-creation counter
for its already-total input.

R2-F3 uses a conservative static-only total ceiling, an earlier append-only
control, two projection-preserving keep/state-only phases with immediate warm
history continuity, and a material summary/erase rebuild.  Cold static warming,
repeat keep cache loss, and missing attribution cannot qualify curation.  Parent
acceptance additionally found future baseline/control observations could qualify
past curation; the final classifier consumes a leading chronological control
prefix and rejects late or interleaved controls.

Parent accepted the settled owner correction after source inspection and
11/11 pure classifier/external-record witnesses.  Implementer mechanism tests
passed 45/45 and request composition passed 26/26.  The changed older-model
request witness passed; its broader file's local socket test was blocked by the
sandbox, with no product defect established.  Complete local-socket-enabled and
fresh current-config gates will run on the clean correction commit.  The earlier
=d6cbba6e= gates passed 2,853/2,853 and 9/9, respectively; they do not replace
the new gates or independent Tick.

prefix control observations are consumed before any curation, and later control
phases are rejected.  Evidence after an operation cannot qualify the earlier
operation.  P3's synthetic positive sequence returns =pass=; none of this enables

** Findings

*** TODO Medium/P2 R3-F1: Aggregate evidence drops unknown totals and reports a partial sum as known

F98-C5 requires missing input counters to remain unknown.  The corrected
per-request normalization returns nil when an Anthropic total cannot be
derived, but =e-live-e2e--external-evidence-record= drops those nil values with
=delq nil= at =e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el:1969--1974=.  It then sums remaining
values into =:total-input-tokens= at =2054--2057=.  One known request makes a
partially unknown scenario appear to have a known total.

A credential-free probe uses the actual Anthropic normalizer and actual public
external-record function:

#+begin_example
request 1: input=18000, read=0, creation=0; total=18000
request 2: input=12000, read=18000, creation=unknown; total=unknown
per-request normalized totals: (18000 nil)
record :total-input-tokens: 18000
#+end_example

The true scenario sum is unknown.  This silently omits the second request rather
than preserving the missing total.  The new per-request helper is correct;
the invalidated aggregate consumer is not.  Parent independently confirmed the
source composition.  The reproducer is in
=/private/tmp/e-feature98/review3-evidence-probe.el=.

Smallest owning correction: require all supplied required usage totals to be
numeric before producing the aggregate, mirroring the existing all-known
=cached-token-values= rule.  Otherwise emit =unavailable=.  Preserve the raw
usage and per-request unknowns.  Add a mixed-known/missing Anthropic-total public
record witness, plus all-known and all-missing boundaries.  No production usage
mapping, durable state, registry, or retry policy is needed.

** Integration defect disposition and current evidence

The post-Tick configured gateway attempt exposed a repository evidence-path
integration defect: =e-live-e2e--activity-of-type= tried to read a process-local
aggregate from an async SQLite session.  The durable review records four
dispatched requests and a subsequent collector failure; no P2 result or semantic
success is carried from that attempt.  Its failure does not constitute provider
cache benefit or a controlled provider-contract contradiction.

The defect is closed at =e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el:368--392=.  Async stores now use
=e-session-async-record-page= with =record-type "activity-event"=, oldest-first
order, limit 100, and the returned =:next= position as the next =:after= cursor.
The memory-store path retains the prior local aggregate/filter behavior.

Independent consequential seam checks:

- =e-session-async.el:289--313= defines =:after= as the stable journal-position
  cursor for oldest-first pages.  The caller uses that contract without local
  session reconstruction.
- =e-runtime-store-session-worker.el:1161--1282= filters record type in SQLite,
  orders by position ascending, uses a lookahead row and byte bound, returns
  decoded records under =:records= with values under =:value=, and returns the
  last emitted record's position when truncated.  Cursor progression therefore
  remains correct for row-bound and byte-bound pages and journal gaps.
- The collector filters =:event-type= from decoded values, pushes only matching
  events, and reverses once after pagination.  Nonmatching activity does not
  stop cursor progression, and event chronology is preserved across pages.
- =e-chat-sql-e2e--await= at =e2e/e-chat-sql-e2e-support.el:61= uses the existing
  explicit =e-work-with-batch-await= boundary.  =e-work.el:724= rejects waits
  outside that boundary and in interactive hot paths.  Read failures propagate;
  the collector does not invent fallback counters or cached history.
- Durable collection is a late E2E query.  The public prompt helper observes
  terminal activity and queries durable assistant content; the collection then
  reads durable activity.  The public request/usage join rejects unequal counts,
  so incomplete durable observations cannot qualify cache benefit.  The new
  witness explicitly awaits each append's commit before its dependent query.

The actual public SQL-backed witness at
=e2e/e-cache-efficiency-e2e-test.el:132= creates a disposable async store,
commits 102 token-usage events and another activity event, forbids any local
aggregate call, and verifies ordinals 0--101 in exact order across the 100-record
page boundary.  The existing joined-initial-control witness proves the resulting
chronological evidence interface still distinguishes static warming, missing
control, one/two advances and genuine normalized cold misses.

** Architecture self-check

1. The stopping point repairs supported OpenAI policy and affinity, with honest
   evidence attribution; historical Board cause and external reuse stay separate.
2. It moves toward capability-owned reuse on every subturn with semantic context
   projection retained as independent truth.
3. Provider affinity is localized and reversible. Pinned source and field
   acceptance support the selected mechanism; hit rate remains uncertain.
4. The existing OpenAI profile/request owner selects policy and feeds its body,
   headers and diagnostics. Core session/lifetime ownership is unchanged.
5. One narrow request-derived identity joins existing consumers; no public cache
   control, cross-component manager or registry is introduced.
6. Core still depends on backend semantics, with no new concrete-provider edge.
7. Provider/network effects remain in existing adapters and isolated runners;
   affinity selection is pure.
8. Selection lives for one request and derives from the existing durable session
   identity. No new persistence, schema or migration is added. Test capture state
   is private to its scenario and indexed by ordinary session.
9. Production selection and presence checks are constant in conversation length.
   Existing request serialization still processes history; native item hashing
   is evidence-fixture work only. No cache-cost improvement is assumed.
10. Ordinary replay and semantic projection are preserved; supported affinity
    grants neither continuation nor curation.
11. Effective unknown endpoints retain compatible caching. The obsolete underscore
    header is removed rather than retained as a compatibility switch.
12. The dotted-only generation assumption and missing known Codex affinity are
    removed; retired caller policy controls remain removed.
13. Expected unsupported identity/capability stays implicit locally. Unexpected
    provider/validation errors remain visible; no retry or swallowing is added.
14. Pure profile and injected request/transport checks prove behavior independently
    of providers; complete gates, independent Tick and fresh P1/P2 remain separate.

No new owner, architectural deferral or glossary term is introduced. Provider
adapter use requires an operator-managed Emacs restart; the user's running Emacs,
Board, live store and services were not reloaded, restarted or changed.

*** TODO Medium/P2 R8-F1: Equivalent first-party Responses endpoints and public aliases silently lose supported cache policy

F98-C1/C2 require selection from effective API capability.  =plan.org:168--170=
assigns that capability to endpoint/model/auth, and =210= requires the effective
endpoint/model boundary.  The admitted Codex design at =268--294= also requires
the same policy for aliases reaching the same known first-party API.

The new Codex affinity predicate at
=lisp/adapters/openai/e-openai-profile.el:658--664= compares only the base
=https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex=.  Both public Codex constructors at
=lisp/adapters/openai/e-openai.el:696--722= first call =e-openai-codex-url= when
a base URL is supplied, producing the complete =.../codex/responses= URL.
That full endpoint is accepted by ordinary dispatch at
=lisp/adapters/openai/e-openai-responses.el:706--736=, but fails affinity
selection.  Explicitly configuring the same supported endpoint therefore
removes both the body key and header.

calls the actual selector.  A second probe drives the public
=e-openai-codex-backend-create= and =e-backend-stream-batch= with synthetic
managed-auth accessors and an injected requester; it performs no provider call:

#+begin_example
effective Responses endpoints equal: t
base /codex selection: ordinary-session
constructor-expanded /codex/responses selection: nil
actual public-constructor URL: wss://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses
actual serialized prompt_cache_key present: nil
actual session-id header present: nil
#+end_example

The affected public OpenAI policy has the same endpoint-spelling defect.
At =e-openai-profile.el:464--510=, capability, evidence and older-model routing
use strict base equality.  With =provider=openai= and =model=gpt-6-sol=, these
two accepted inputs dispatch to the identical endpoint:

#+begin_example
https://api.openai.com/v1
  actual URL: wss://api.openai.com/v1/responses
  mode: implicit-plus-stable-guard; guards: 1
  evidence: first-party-responses-documented
https://api.openai.com/v1/responses
  actual URL: wss://api.openai.com/v1/responses
  mode: implicit; guards: 0
  evidence: endpoint-default-unverified
#+end_example

Those public capability/evidence/routing functions additionally check the
provider label =openai=.  A configured token-auth Responses alias at the exact
known first-party base and same model dispatches to the same URL, yet receives
=implicit=, zero guards and =endpoint-default-unverified=.  The built-in label
receives the documented stronger policy.  This is an existing capability-driven
contract violation in a dependency reopened by the generation correction.
The existing custom-provider negative fixtures use third-party endpoints; they
do not justify downgrading this known first-party alias.

Smallest owning correction: recognize the effective known Responses endpoint
consistently in the existing profile owner, and derive public first-party policy
from endpoint, wire API, auth and model rather than catalogue spelling.  Apply
that decision consistently to capability, routing and evidence; retain the
single Codex identity selection feeding both wire outputs.  Preserve managed
auth for Codex, token-auth applicability for the public API, and unsupported
API/third-party gates.  Do not introduce broad host/path/query equivalences or
weaken unrelated context-capability admission.  Add composed witnesses for the
public Codex constructors, supported base/full-endpoint spellings and public
first-party aliases, alongside the existing unsupported endpoint/API/auth and
missing-identity negatives.  Recheck material layout/anchor fencing when these
previously downgraded requests select their supported policy.  No caller cache
option, registry or durable cache state is needed.

The parent independently verified and accepted the constructor, endpoint and
public-alias counterexamples.  They establish omitted supported policy; they
do not establish a particular cache hit-rate loss or explain the Board's
4,608-token ceiling.

#+title: Feature 98 partial P3 acceptance review
#+startup: overview

* Decision and reviewed identity

Four confirmed actionable findings block the candidate runner's external
qualification use.  This is a targeted pre-acceptance review of settled partial
implementation, not a fixed-point Tick or a production-capability decision.
The admitted supporting plan remains implementation-plan-fixed-point at
=af6b8d142e93ee9941e632664df4ede1b53d4a70=; none of the findings requires
changing F98-G1/C1--C6, normative P3, or its keep/summary/erase requirements.

Reviewed source HEAD/base: =c8607e8417b60233a0d856cb553e3b33f8ee5cfb= plus the
settled nine-file working change.  Current identity-bearing working blobs:

| Owner | Blob |
|-------+------|
| e2e/e-cache-efficiency-e2e-test.el | 7c6622674645971246cc869f3bf0bed42bb5908c |
| e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el | d4d8b3012986fc685e2a238611b74e98f0188c62 |
| Anthropic adapter | fd3eb533e4ccc0a4993111084ab87ccc0e6a8d0e |
| OpenAI profile owner | bd5c88f368a31884b9ae49753448958a36bee472 |
| OpenAI adapter | 665e2ecaa69406bba85c4bc62817dcce1acc2072 |
| Dedicated Messages candidate config | 5f1c65c355a759f661ea295cb0601f0b3ee1dfe6 |

* Ordered findings and smallest owning corrections

** TODO P3-AR1 High/P1: contaminated assertions do not prove native projection

The live prompts at =e-cache-efficiency-e2e-test.el:2619--2630= repeat the exact
kept facts, raw summary source, raw erased source, and replacement summary in
ordinary user history.  The tool emits the same sentinel facts at
=1755--1777=.  At =1779--1782=, the presence helper searches the printed
provider-neutral =:messages= field, despite its docstring calling this native
history.  The live assertions at =2747--2763= use that helper.

Consequently the raw summary/erase absence checks fail even when the intended
tool source is correctly removed: their original user instructions still
contain =P3-SUMMARY-RAW-THREE= and =P3-ERASE-RAW-FOUR=.  Conversely a retained
user instruction can satisfy a keep/summary presence check even if the actual
native request loses the curated fact.  Checking neutral messages also leaves
a serialization/replay defect in captured =:native-body= undetected.

Keep opaque source keys in user instructions and refer to the returned source
label without repeating the fact or raw sentinel.  Put the unique raw facts
only in synthetic tool results.  Assert against the captured actual native
Messages/Responses history after the genuine fresh-projection boundary.
Distinguish expected same-turn replay from the later projected history; do
not erase or weaken mandatory replay to make this test pass.  Verify that a
kept fact survives, the summary replaces its unique raw source, and the erased
unique source disappears.  Do not rely only on a final OK marker.

Minimum proof: run the actual constructors through a credential-free public
SQLite curation composition for both native shapes, with uncontaminated
prompts.  A deliberately stale native raw source must fail even if neutral
messages are correct; a deliberately missing native kept/summary fact must
also fail.  Same-turn raw replay and later projected absence must be separately
identified by actual request IDs.

** TODO P3-AR2 High/P1: whole-group prefix hashes cannot prove keep preservation

At =2120--2139=, the prefix helper hashes the longest common native-item prefix
within the supplied group.  At =2154--2155= the control group's hash becomes
the initial history fingerprint.  At =2196--2197= a fresh later turn gets a
new independently measured common-prefix hash, then =2205--2208= supplies the
previous group's hash as the curation's before value.  The classifier at
=e-live-e2e-test.el:1547--1567= correctly requires equal before/after
fingerprints for a projection-preserving keep.

Even an ideal keep with unchanged old history normally appends new turns
between these groups.  For example control item sequences =[A B]= and
=[A B C]= hash prefix =[A B]=, while fresh sequences =[A B C D]=,
=[A B C D E]= and =[A B C D E F]= hash =[A B C D]=.  Existing =[A B]= is
unchanged, but the producer submits different hashes and gets
=projection-unconfirmed=.  Giving every request the same newly computed
group hash does not establish continuity with the previously warm history.

Measure an explicit preexisting, previously warmed ordered native prefix
and its boundary/count before the transition.  Compare those same positions
and content after the keep, allowing appended new facts outside that protected
region.  For summary/erase, bind the affected source/projection boundary to
the accepted package and record the new reusable history actually observed.
Retain complete wire hashes separately from a defined semantic projection
digest; moving cache-control metadata must not silently redefine the protected
history.  If the actual native prefix changes or loses reuse, preserve that
negative rather than manufacture equality.

Minimum proof: an unchanged protected prefix with ordinary append growth
passes attribution; changing, deleting or reordering a protected item fails.
The witness must include the real before/after native requests and boundary
indices, rather than injected equal strings.  Repeated keep/state cycles and
both material transitions must still satisfy the unchanged conservative
cache-read predicate.

** TODO P3-AR3 High/P1: selected evidence drops requests and fabricates identity

The evidence assembler at =2141--2225= emits one baseline, the append-control
requests, and the first later top-level turn for each package.  Every request
in the curation turns is omitted, including source reads, the curation request,
and mandatory same-turn replay.  Extra/interim scenarios also disappear.
Each emitted usage gets the single baseline =identity= rather than its own
captured identity; =2227--2233= copies only counters.  Thus an endpoint/model/
static-prefix mismatch on a later request cannot reach the classifier, and a
failed/missing-counter/static-only request in an omitted turn cannot affect
its result.

The live caller at =2698--2714= invokes the classifier before its later
complete-join semantic assertion.  The existing join predicate at =528--556=
can reject missing/extra/ambiguous attribution, but it does not control this
classification call.  A finish can be matched yet failed: the finish join
records its status at =2060--2063=, while the complete-join predicate checks
matching, not successful finish status.  Preserve semantic success and unknown
counters separately; neither turns incomplete evidence into a positive.

The content-free emitted report at =2768--2812= retains joined request rows,
but only package counts, projection names and fresh-request IDs.  It omits
accepted curation IDs, response-entry/consumption joins, exact classifier
events, and measured before/after boundaries.  An independent consumer cannot
reconstruct the asserted curation-to-request relation from this artifact.
This also means that retaining raw rows in the report does not remedy dropping
them from the classifier.

Use one chronological provenance-preserving evidence stream containing every
actual request and observed curation event.  Bind accepted package identity,
response/consumer request, consumed frame, turn, and first fresh request using
durable identities.  Distinguish same-turn replay, first fresh projection and
subsequent reuse phases without suppressing intervening requests.  Copy each
request's own identity, authentic provider ordinal, joined counters and finish
status.  Make complete attribution/success/native semantic proof a prerequisite
for a positive diagnostic result.  Unexpected, missing, failed or ambiguous
observations must remain conservative.

Emit the exact content-bounded evidence inputs and curation/boundary join
records used by that decision, in addition to all raw counters/hashes.  Preserve
unknowns as unknowns and retain a distinct semantic result.  No prompt, tool
content, auth token or header value is needed.

Minimum proof: extra same-turn request, failed finish, missing usage, duplicate
curation/response/consumer identity, mismatched endpoint/model/static digest,
and an unjoined extra usage all block a positive.  A realistic synthetic
native/public SQLite transcript with both summary and erase must reconstruct
the same result from its serialized evidence.  Include an omitted-request
cold/static counterexample so selected warm requests cannot hide inefficiency.

** TODO P3-AR4 Medium/P2: wrapper availability is reported as confirmed capability

At =2701--2706=, =capability-confirmed= means only that the private wrapper
granted at least once and every captured request offers the curation tool.
That value gates the classifier at =2711--2714= and is emitted as
=:capability-confirmed t= at =2784=.  The record separately says
=:candidate-authority "ert-exact-route-wrapper"= and
=:production-qualification "unavailable"=, but the confirmed label still
assigns qualification authority to mere availability.  Plan step 3 explicitly
requires distinct candidate authority, effective carrier availability and
production qualification, and says a wrapper grant is not efficiency evidence.

Represent this boolean as carrier availability or candidate carrier admission.
Make the diagnostic predicate's prerequisite explicitly availability, with a
separate result for measured candidate efficiency and a separate production
qualification status.  A positive candidate measurement can support the next
unpublished normal-code acceptance candidate; it cannot make current production
qualified.  Keep all ordinary production declarations none.

Minimum proof: a granted carrier with zero/missing/static-only reads reports
available plus unconfirmed/negative efficiency, never confirmed capability.
A candidate pass remains diagnostic and cannot restore production availability;
the wrapper-free, exact-revision P3 stage and independent evidence closure are
still required.

* Targeted coverage and current source dispositions

| Cell | Current disposition |
|------+---------------------|
| Existing F98 authority and both material projection requirements | Cleared/carried. No contract change admitted; classifier now requires at least two keep cycles plus both summary and erase. |
| Trusted adapter identity plumbing | Cleared for this targeted source review. Both existing owners derive endpoint, resolved model, auth/API/transport/requester; Anthropic backend/default/base/requester propagation and OpenAI native derivation replace independent hardcoded wire selection. |
| Caller controls and normal publication | Cleared. Normal owners still return carrier none. Private wrapper equality grants only in the ERT process; copied caller carrier fields alone do not grant. No core policy, persistent registry or public opt-out is added. |
| Direct native candidate composition | Cleared for tool availability only. Credential-free constructor tests check native tool publication and route/model negatives. This proves fields/publication, not native curation reuse or cache benefit. |
| Real public curation lifecycle | Partially cleared. Synthetic SQLite test uses real admission/package/consumption paths and correctly distinguishes same-turn raw replay from fresh neutral projection. Actual native projection proof remains blocked by AR1. |
| Fresh top-level boundary helper | Cleared at this limited boundary. It locates consumption by response ID, skips all requests of that curation turn and selects a later turn. AR3 requires stronger unique accepted identity joins and complete chronology around it. |
| Repeated-keep preservation attribution | Candidate/confirmed finding AR2. |
| Complete phase/counter/identity classification and external reconstructability | Candidate/confirmed finding AR3. |
| Candidate availability versus qualification | Candidate/confirmed finding AR4. |
| Native projection semantics | Candidate/confirmed finding AR1. |
| External P3 / normal-code P3 / production capability | Unknown/unrun; ordinary production carrier remains none. |
| Unchanged ordinary caching/core lifetime semantics | Carried; no selected source delta changes those owners. No new ordinary benefit or historical Board causality claim follows. |

* Architecture disposition

The existing adapter capability owners are the correct location for identity
and native wire selection.  The P3 runner/evidence helpers own all four
corrections; none requires another policy owner, core semantic change, registry
or public configuration control.  Request-local identity, captures and counters
have disposable test-process/session lifetime.  The source does not introduce
a production positive selector.

The fourteen repository self-checks retain prior whole-scope dispositions,
with this incremental delta: (1) this stopping point improves trusted plumbing
but cannot yet supply valid P3 qualification; (2) direction remains automatic
compatible caching plus qualified curation; (3) route qualification is uncertain
and isolated; (4) existing adapters and E2E evidence remain owners; (5) interfaces
stay narrow, with AR2/AR3 correcting producer-consumer coupling; (6) dependency
direction stays core-to-capability, not core-to-provider; (7) provider effects
remain outward; (8) added state is disposable request/test evidence; (9) bounded
diagnostic capture cost is appropriate, but avoiding necessary observations is
not an optimization; (10) AR1--AR4 restore actual semantic/provenance substitution;
(11) ordinary production unavailability and requester fences remain compatible;
(12) remove misleading confirmed terminology/selected evidence shortcuts;
(13) AR3 must retain failed/incomplete observations rather than bypass them;
(14) native/public credential-free compositions must prove the corrected runner
before provider diagnostics, with focused tests changing at their owning seams.

* Verification and external boundary

Read-only source/diff/working-blob inspection supplied the proofs above.
No canonical suite, provider call, live Emacs/store/Board operation, repository
edit or Git write was performed; only this private artifact was authored.
Prior ordinary gates do not establish the new partial P3 producer.
Provider cache efficiency, endpoint acceptance for curation, and complete
wrapper-free P3 remain unresolved external questions.  No production positive,
fixed-point Tick, full-feature Done or cache-efficiency claim is justified.

log-read: none.

* P3 runner remediation: parent acceptance checkpoint 2026-10-07

The four P3-AR findings are remediated in the existing E2E evidence owners.
Candidate authority remains private ERT scope; normal adapter carriers remain
none. Native semantics inspect system/instructions and input/messages, with
opaque source instructions. The public disposable SQLite witness decodes a
native Responses curation envelope, commits all four real packages, and checks
both native serializers, including stale/missing projection negatives.

Every native request is retained in chronological classification, with its own
route identity, authentic ordinal, successful finish and complete usage join.
The runner captures the preflight response/provider/frame consumer relation;
package fields come from accepted promotion/erasure components. Pure erasure
has no promotion ID in the consumed event and is joined by its actual response,
frame and consumer identities. Source keys resolve from provider-assigned tool
calls. Semantic item digests exclude cache metadata; protected boundaries are
measured from actually shared preceding warm requests and compared at the same
positions, allowing later appended history. Cold interim requests cannot be
hidden by later warm requests. Candidate carrier availability, measured
candidate efficiency and production qualification remain distinct.

Focused validation: 5 P3 tests passed, 1 live test skipped without E_E2E.
This includes both native shapes through public SQLite, 21 complete request
joins, four accepted packages and content-bounded evidence roundtrip; fixed
prefix append/change/delete/reorder witnesses; failed/duplicate/extra finish,
mixed route and hidden cold interim negatives. Broad exact-revision gates and
independent source closure are pending. No provider has been called yet.

The previous implementation attempts were partial, not acceptance. Parent
finished the form boundaries, response joins, actual native body coverage and

registry or production selector changed. The prior fourteen architectural

unchanged. Adapter source use requires a restart, which was not performed.

#+title: Feature 98 Review 14
#+startup: overview

* Review 14: Independent partial P3 source Tick

** Decision and exact identity

Three confirmed actionable findings prevent =source-fixed-point= and prevent
admission of this runner as a trustworthy external P3 qualification producer.
The original P3-AR1--AR4 corrections are established at their owning seams;
their changed dependents reveal the three findings below.  No normative

Implementation comparison is =c8607e84..ed3f5eac=, with unchanged adapter
pre-implementation source baseline =cda222a7= and admitted plan identity
=af6b8d142e93ee9941e632664df4ede1b53d4a70=.
F98-G1/C1--C6, DP1 and normative P3 remain the authority.
Prior whole-scope production/cache/core proof carries except where the new
capability identity helper or P3 evidence producer invalidates a dependency.

** Findings, in correction order

*** TODO R14-F1 High/P1: extra post-curation requests can lose reusable history and still pass

Owner: =e2e/e-live-e2e-test.el:1695--1710=, with the corresponding P3 evidence
producer/consumer tests.

After two post-curation read advances, =pair-counted= becomes true.
The next request's cached-read predicate uses =(or pair-counted ...)=,
bypassing both further advancement and continuity with the previous request.
It requires only the original static ceiling plus granularity.
=last-reusable-read= also stops updating after that pair, so a later curation
can use an older, smaller floor despite newer observed reusable history.

Independent pure actual-classifier proof at this exact revision:
the existing identity/provenance-complete fixture returns =pass=.
Append one copied final erase-reuse event with a fresh request ID/ordinal,
same route/turn/history, total input =63000= and read =4224=.
The stronger variant also appends a native semantic item and changes the
native/history hashes, preserving the prior shared prefix; it likewise passes.
The preceding event has total =62000= and read =12288=; there is no new

** Findings resolved during this plan review

All four material candidates were independently sourced, parent-verified,
corrected by the parent and read back in the identity above. They carry as
specific implementation-review risks, not as proof that code is already fixed.

1. R24-PF1, non-message anchor feasibility: =e-loop.el:1198–1230= allocates an
   audit-only response identity when a reserved curation response has no
   assistant text. =e-session-aggregate.el:1847–1861= persists it as activity
   rather than a transcript message. Ordinary tool responses may also anchor
   a tool-call entry, excluded by the durable filter at =1481–1501=.
   The initial plan did not explicitly cover these insertion positions.
   Closed: resolve anchors in the selected canonical journal path before
   semantic filtering; no synthetic message is introduced. R2/R3 require
   ordinary-message, audit-only and filtered tool-call anchor evidence.

2. R24-PF2, continuation identity independent of availability:
   =e-openai-profile.el:591–605= includes the current curation identity only
   when the carrier is available; =576–589= can leave older/flattened wire
   layouts without a revision. Accepted journals still render when the
   carrier is unavailable. Bumping only that gated presentation identity
   would not fence every changed retained-data layout.
   Closed: retained role/order/framing identity applies independently of
   carrier availability and optional segmented/cache controls, in the existing
   identity owner. R3 proves old-layout rejection and new-layout compatibility
   on candidate, unavailable and older/flattened Responses profiles.

3. R24-PF3, partial compaction ownership: =e-compaction.el:438–487= captures
   all active promotion/curation records while selecting only a durable prefix;
   =595–615= absorbs the flattened list. Session projection at
   =e-session-aggregate.el:1575–1600= currently filters by active generation.
   An uncovered retained item must neither move before its anchor into the
   compacted prefix nor vanish when generation ownership changes.
   Closed: partition by canonical anchor relative to the covered boundary;
   absorb covered items exactly once; preserve later role/content/anchor/order
   and eligibility, including older-generation records, without journal
   rewriting. Existing optimistic frontier/staleness checks remain. R3 requires
   before/at/after-boundary compaction, reopen and fork evidence.

4. R24-PF4, detached anchor producer: the selected SQLite query at
   =e-runtime-store-session-worker.el:2019–2159= builds all path IDs internally
   but returns messages/indexes and generation/promotion/package records,
   omitting audit-only activity response positions. The harness cannot recover
   those positions from its existing bounded payload.
   Closed: DP1-R1 explicitly includes this existing producer. It supplies
   bounded content-free anchor positions before filtering under current
   selected-branch/record/byte limits and lazy aggregate behavior. R2 requires
   real SQLite audit-only detached parity without loading the aggregate.

** Contract and behavioral design coverage

| Cell | Plan-level disposition | Source/acceptance join |
|------+------------------------+-----------------------|

The smallest faithful alternative requires both correcting the retained-data
role and fixing response-anchored chronology. A role-only change leaves the
current harness/fork/compaction append-at-tail movement. Anchoring alone leaves
Anthropic's system-channel relocation. Rewriting all system messages would
change genuine instructions. A core-owned portable data role, shared pure
ordering and adapter-local encoding preserve the intended semantics with
clear ownership and no policy registry.

The reference-data framing is native presentation, not mutation of canonical
record values or promotion of data into new instructions. Responses HTTP/WS
share translation; Chat Completions and Anthropic use their text encoders.
Structured values must become supported native text rather than leaking an
unsupported native role/content shape. Existing system/developer instructions
and request-local source markers retain their channels/roles. Old system-only
checkpoints retain their roles because provenance cannot safely be inferred.

** Architecture self-check, all fourteen questions

1. Useful stopping point: corrected replay/order/native semantics improve the
   repository without requiring capability publication.
2. Direction: retained reference data remains inline and stable across future
   appends, with provider-native behavior delegated to adapters.
3. Stable/uncertain decisions: semantic role/order are core-owned; native
   framing and unknown external cache benefit are local and separately tested.
4. Ownership: codec owns values/role; session owns chronology; query owns bounded
   detached facts; harness composes; adapters encode native data.
5. Coupling/interfaces: one pure ordering operation prevents divergent
   normal/detached/fork/compaction derivations; query payload stays consumer-shaped.
6. Dependency direction: core semantics do not depend on provider implementations;
   adapter mapping points toward the portable core role.
7. Side effects: existing journal/app service boundaries remain; projection and
   framing are derived, and the review proposes no new mutation path.
8. State/lifetime: no journal rewrite or cache registry; portable checkpoints
   persist the semantic role, while ordering/framing rebuild per request.
9. Performance: reuse benefit remains unproved; existing bounded query work
   must stay lazy. No unbounded aggregate load or performance regression is
   justified by convenience. New native encoding cost is measured in review.
10. Substitution: all shipped native adapters consume the same data role and
    exact values without emitting unsupported roles or narrowing content types.
11. Compatibility: v2/v3 journals remain readable; new checkpoints retain the
    new role; old opaque system checkpoints are not guessed/reclassified.
12. Legacy removal: appended-tail reconstruction and obsolete system-role
    retention assertions are replaced; required read compatibility remains.
13. Errors: unresolved eligible anchors and incompatible layout state fail
    explicitly; provider/credential failure cannot grant capability.
14. Proof: R1–R4 cover pure values, actual SQLite/native composition, persistence,
    partial compaction and continuation before external R5.

** Downstream implementation and external boundaries

credential-free gates are future acceptance requirements, not already-passing
claims. Carry the four source risks above into independent implementation
review, especially genuine non-message anchors, older-generation uncovered

Existing Opus repeated keep relocation/cache recreation and the earlier Codex

The later controlled Codex record remains conservative nonqualification with

those records or guarantee that native correction will qualify either route.
Unconfirmed routes retain no carrier; a candidate diagnostic alone cannot
publish availability. Any restoration follows the separately reviewed exact
normal-source wrapper-free P3 sequence. Public Anthropic/OpenAI endpoints need
their own evidence; gateway/Codex results cannot qualify them.

=ded7697680a7a0c64a263d79d65a53d02a612a5a=; original implementation scope base
=4f042f184eb2290bc05f452e0f4b550e48328766=. The intervening committed change
touches separate tool-output owners. SHA-256 of =git diff --binary -- lisp test=:
=c6d9567f1d7c52f5d872ca715eb27d0d699b513b1ad595e352ba1158478f8913=.
The supporting plan SHA-256 was independently read back as
=e4959fb16ba5e3ac873c0bc07596fc6da18720d3bc0b7e59691bdec89548879a=.
F98-G1/C1–C6 and DP1 retain their existing normative authority. Review 24's
plan-level admission is unchanged; its implementation risks are rebaselined
below. The parent's v2 realization clarification applies canonical =:value=
projection to readable v2 records, without changing record/provenance authority.

All nine changed production owners and their changed test seams were inspected:
lifetime codec/weave, aggregate projection/fork, harness projection/identity,
bounded SQLite selected-path producer, portable compaction, Anthropic,
Responses, Chat Completions and OpenAI profile layout identity. Parent-owned
status/admission prose is not implementation proof. No repository edit, Git
write, provider request or user runtime/store access occurred. Only private
review artifacts and disposable synthetic stores were written.

** Ordered findings

*** R25-F1 — Medium/P2: v2 reference projection changes canonical content

=e-context-lifetime.el:2155–2168= changes the model role to =context= but
still formats ="Promoted fact %s: %s"= and applies =prin1-to-string= to
structured values. Internal fact IDs and Lisp representation therefore enter
the model-facing content. This violates retained-context plan R1/F98-C3:
readable v2 and v3 projections preserve canonical content, with provenance
remaining in the record and adapter-native framing applied separately.

The independent actual codec/projection witness produced:
#+begin_example
v2 canonical=(:enabled :json-false)
projection=((:role context
  :content "Promoted fact internal-fact-id: (:enabled :json-false)"))
#+end_example
Thus the adapter receives an already mutated string and cannot encode the
canonical object as JSON. Existing v2 string-based fixtures still prescribe
that inherited presentation; they do not justify compatibility with it.

Smallest owning correction: =e-context-lifetime-promotion-fact-messages=
projects each canonical =:value= directly with =:role context=, as v3 exact
items do. Keep IDs/provenance in records. Strengthen v2 structured codec,
normal/detached/native, checkpoint/reopen and fork assertions to verify exact
values and exclude IDs/Lisp formatting from native reference text. Do not
rewrite old system-only checkpoints or stored records.

*** R25-F2 — Medium/P2: legacy compaction truncates the portable uncovered tail

=e-session-aggregate.el:1542–1546= selects the legacy compacted suffix before
applying the portable generation boundary at =1596–1614=. The SQLite query
also returns messages and generation/promotion/package records only after
the legacy =first-kept-entry-id= cut (=e-runtime-store-session-worker.el:
2100–2148=). An applicable portable boundary can be earlier: the existing
=exclude-entry-ids= preparation deliberately stops the portable covered prefix
before an excluded durable entry (=e-compaction.el:313–337, 414–428=).
The actual harness commits both the legacy compaction record and the portable
generation (=e-harness-turn.el:778–806=). Keeping only the legacy suffix loses
portable-uncovered messages; detached projection additionally loses retained
records whose older-generation anchors lie in that uncovered interval.

The independent disposable SQLite public lifecycle used real
=e-compaction-prepare :portable t :exclude-entry-ids '("excluded")=,
preflight, =e-session-append-compaction=, portable boundary application and
=session-context-path=. It produced:
#+begin_example
boundaries=(:legacy "first-kept" :portable "covered")
normal/reopened tail:
  ((context "AFTER-BOUNDARY") (user "kept"))
detached tail:
  ((user "kept"))
canonical portable-context builder:
  ((system "C1") (user "excluded durable intent")
   (assistant "later durable answer") (context "AFTER-BOUNDARY")
   (user "kept"))
#+end_example
The fact was authored in the old generation, anchored after ="covered"=,
and never absorbed in =C1=. The normal and detached paths both violate R3/
F98-C3, and disagree with each other. Closing/reopening the SQLite store
preserves the normal loss. The explicit-head fork correctly includes all
uncovered content in its checkpoint; that separate successful route does
not clear the normal/detached failures.

Smallest owning correction: aggregate and selected-path query must use the
applicable accepted portable boundary to govern the semantic lifetime tail;
the legacy first-kept cut cannot supersede it. Preserve legacy-only behavior
where no applicable portable replacement exists. Return eligible older-generation
retention by selected canonical anchor, with existing branch/record/byte bounds

** Architecture self-check, all fourteen questions

1. Useful stopping point: coherent retained data/order/replay is useful while
   capability stays unavailable; the current three defects block that point.
2. Direction: core reference data stays after its response anchor and adapters
   translate it inline instead of relocating it into instruction channels.
3. Stable/uncertain decisions: role, canonical value and journal chronology are
   stable semantics; native framing/cache benefit remain adapter-local and
   externally qualified. No speculative extension point is added.
4. Ownership: lifetime codec/weave, session chronology, bounded query, harness
   composition and native adapter encoding retain cohesive responsibilities.
5. Coupling/interfaces: shared pure weave reduces divergent derivations; SQL
   positions are consumer-shaped. F2 is a boundary-selection inconsistency
   within those existing owners, not a reason for a second registry.
6. Dependency direction: adapters depend on portable semantics; core has no
   provider/UI/transport dependency introduced by this change.
7. Side effects: ordering and encoding are pure; existing journal and atomic
   package/compaction/fork mutations remain at their established owners.
8. State/lifetime: durable records are not rewritten. Context roles persist in
   new checkpoints; request ordering/framing rebuild from records. No cache
   state or runtime qualification store is introduced.
9. Performance: ordering now sorts selected durable/retained events; anchor
   maps require selected-path work already bounded on SQLite. Bounds/lazy
   access remain essential to F2's fix. No latency or cache benefit has been
   measured or justified by implementation field presence.
10. Substitution: all native routes accept the same context role and structured
    values. Normal/detached substitution currently fails in F2; fork semantic
    substitution fails in F3.
11. Compatibility: v2 journals remain readable, but F1 must preserve canonical
    values. Old system-only checkpoints stay untouched; layout identity rejects
    old anchors and permits matching new layouts independently of availability.
12. Legacy removal: obsolete v2 ID/Lisp presentation must be removed; legacy
    compaction remains for compatible legacy-only consumers, without cutting
    an applicable portable tail. No compatibility option is needed.
13. Errors: eligible missing/untyped anchors reject invalid projection data;
    covered records may be omitted; existing admission/preflight errors remain.
    F2/F3 currently lose data silently and need owner corrections.
14. Tests: codec, pure weave, bounded SQL and native constructor tests isolate
    owners. The independent probes reveal missing real partial-compaction and
    initial-empty-generation fork relations; add those durable regressions and
    preserve the native later-append relation, then run required full gates.

** R32-F1: Provider symbol incorrectly determines effective capability

Priority: Medium/P2. Contracts: F98-C6, effective-capability selection under
C1/C2 and plan.org step 5. Owner:
=lisp/adapters/anthropic/e-anthropic.el:1243–1253=;
the new test at =test/e-anthropic-test.el:911–1025= prescribes the defect.

The candidate matcher compares the entire identity against a quoted plist
containing =:provider eng-ai-gateway-opus-5-5=. The new test deliberately maps
both that symbol and =unreviewed-provider-alias= to the identical trusted
profile and asserts the alias gets =none=. Thus two normal backend/native
requests to the identical resolved endpoint/model/auth/API/transport/requester
have different availability solely because the configuration symbol differs.
Changing the display name is tested, but that does not establish provider-symbol
independence.

Independent credential-free public composition reproduced:

#+begin_example
provider=eng-ai-gateway-opus-5-5
backend-carrier=context-curate-wire
endpoint=https://eng-ai-model-gateway.sfproxy.devx-preprod.aws-esvc1-useast2.aws.sfdc.cl/v1/messages
native-model=claude-opus-5-5 native-curation=t

provider=review-equivalent-alias
backend-carrier=none
endpoint=https://eng-ai-model-gateway.sfproxy.devx-preprod.aws-esvc1-useast2.aws.sfdc.cl/v1/messages
native-model=claude-opus-5-5 native-curation=nil
#+end_example

Both profiles shared the same endpoint/auth/model declarations and synthetic
environment-token source. Options included copied carrier/capability fields;
they did not change either result. No provider request was dispatched.

This is not useful unknown-route conservatism: none of the trusted effective
dimensions changed. F98-C6 selects an effective adapter/API/model capability;
step 5 restricts the tested effective combination, and plan.org:531 explicitly
states provider labels cannot supply capability proof. Provider identity remains
useful provenance but must not become a substitute capability dimension.

Smallest correction: keep this decision in the existing adapter owner; compare
the tested resolved endpoint/model/auth/API/transport/requester fields rather
than the cosmetic provider symbol. Preserve the provider field in diagnostics
and retain every genuinely unknown or changed effective-field rejection. Invert
the equivalent-alias assertion and prove it through both ordinary backend
capability and actual native constructor/tool serialization, alongside the
existing wrong-endpoint/model/auth/requester/injected negatives. Do not broaden
endpoint equivalence, add a policy option or grant an unsupported route.

** R32-F2: Supplied resolved model is prefixed a second time

Priority: Medium/P2. Contracts: F98-C6/C4 effective model re-evaluation and
step 5 exact normal-source identity. Owner:
=e-anthropic--context-capability-identity= at =e-anthropic.el:1215–1241=,
with consequential callers at =2671–2697= and =2734–2754=.

capability owner. The identity helper calls that resolver again on the supplied

empty prefix, so they cannot distinguish these representations.

gateway endpoint/auth, with =:model-prefix "claude-"= and raw option model
="opus-5-5"=. It reproduced both helper and actual compositions:

#+begin_example
resolved="claude-opus-5-5"
identity-model="claude-claude-opus-5-5"
carrier=none
prefixed-option backend-carrier=none
native-model="claude-opus-5-5" native-curation=nil
#+end_example

The native body uses the once-resolved canonical model, while the candidate

Smallest correction: document and enforce the existing shared representation
boundary. A supplied effective =:model= is already resolved and must be used
directly; only a raw options/profile/backend-default fallback is resolved once.
Keep actual wire model and backend/direct-request identity equal. Audit the
